--- Commit Message ---
LU-18857 mgs: add timeout mechanism for allow_register

Add automatic timeout functionality to the allow_register
parameter to improve security by limiting the window during
which new filesystems or targets can register.

Values
  0          registration disabled, initial default
  unlimited  registration enabled indefinitely, no timer
  N[s|m|h]   registration enabled for N seconds/minutes/hours,
             default unit is hours when no unit is given

When a timed value is set, a timer disables registration on expiry.
show() reports remaining time with an explicit 's' unit.

Timed windows above 7 days are rejected with -EINVAL.
Use 'unlimited' for an open-ended window.

Automatic default window
 - allow_register defaults to 0, disabled
 - the first target to register for an unknown filesystem opens a
   one-time 24-hour window, but only on a truly fresh MGS with no
   existing filesystem config log yet, unless the admin already set
   allow_register explicitly via module parameter or lctl set_param
 - an MGS already managing at least one filesystem never auto-arms,
   even for a later unknown fsname after a reboot or module reload
 - the window never re-arms again during the same module load
 - suppressing the automatic window entirely requires setting
   allow_register explicitly, e.g. 'options mgs allow_register=0'

Concurrent first registration
 - arming the one-time window is serialized under
   allow_register_mutex. A losing thread waits for the winner to
   finish arming, then rechecks allow_register.

Boot-time module parameter
 - new mgs.allow_register=<value> module parameter applies the same
   policy at module load time via class_allow_register_set(), so it
   gets identical validation, locking, and timer arming as the sysfs
   attribute

Compatibility changes from the original boolean allow_register
 - initial default is now 0 (disabled, see automatic default window
   above) instead of 1 (enabled)
 - allow_register=1 set at runtime means enabled for 1 hour, not
   enabled

Persistent set_param -P
 - allow_register=0 and allow_register=unlimited may be persisted
 - timed values cannot use -P, replaying a duration would open a
   fresh registration window on every mount
 - on config apply, bare 1 (old boolean on) maps to unlimited, other
   non-steady values are rejected

Also
  - mgs_check_target only treats rc > 0 as index in use. Negative
    error codes propagate instead of being overwritten to 0
  - mgs_check_index handles peer-supplied LDD_F_NEED_INDEX and range
    checks the peer-supplied target index
  - test-framework opens a bounded allow_register window around
    target format/add and writeconf
  - Documentation/man4/allow_register.4
  - SEE ALSO allow_register(4) in mkfs.lustre(8) and mount.lustre(8)
  - conf-sanity 157a/157b changed allow_register=1 to 'unlimited'
  - conf-sanity 157c timeout window coverage
  - conf-sanity 157d param persistence and legacy 1 to unlimited
  - conf-sanity 157e mgs module parameter boot-time enforcement
  - conf-sanity 157f auto-arm 24 hour registration window
  - explicitly reopen the allow_register window in test scripts as
    needed
  - mutex around timer/value updates in store/show

Fixes: f96f38c9e1e3 ("LU-18857 mgs: add allow_register tunable parameter")
Signed-off-by: Chakshu Kansal <ckansal@ddn.com>

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-09-10 00:02:51.000000000) ---
Patch Set 37:

(5 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The one-time default window is armed from mgs_find_or_make_fsdb_nolock(), which is used for a lot more than target registration, so it fires (and the -EACCES below it bites) on paths that have nothing to do with a target registering - most visibly the nodemap fsdb. Also, none of the new subtests exercise the automatic 24-hour window itself: 157a/157b/157c/157e all set allow_register explicitly first, which sets allow_register_user_set and suppresses it. Consider a case that starts from an untouched allow_register and checks that the first new filesystem opens a non-zero window, that get_param reports a remaining time, and that a second new filesystem does not re-arm it.

Would it be better to keep the framework reset confined to conf-sanity, or to key it off a variable the 157x tests can turn off?

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Problematic, see inline comments. The automatic one-time window looks unreachable on any MGS, so with the new default of 0 a freshly formatted cluster cannot register its first targets. Test coverage is also missing for that path. conf-sanity test_157f registers a new filesystem on an MGS that already manages `lustre`. The commit message says such an MGS never auto-arms, so the test cannot pass under the documented design either. Could a test be added that formats a fresh MGS, leaves allow_register untouched, and checks that the first MDT registers and that allow_register then shows a timed value? While refreshing, please also look at the man page minors inline.
